# Helios model-training dependency close: contract for the Q3 2025 exposure close

This document is the authority for how the exports in this directory are read and for the exact shape of every deliverable of the close.
Where another export or document seems to say something different about the shape of a deliverable, this document wins. Rules about the
subject matter (which record governs, which source has authority for which field) are stated here and in the documents this contract
names, and those rules decide every conflict between sources.

## 1. The close

The close covers the quarter that starts at 2025-07-01T00:00:00Z and ends at the cut, 2025-10-01T00:00:00Z. The cut is exclusive: an
instant is "before the cut" when it is strictly earlier than 2025-10-01T00:00:00Z. Every statement "as of the cut" means the state
produced by the records recorded before the cut.

The close has three logical layers resolved in memory during one run. The source layer resolves six record values, the risk layer
derives six values from them, and the close layer derives six values from both. Six evidence views are serialised from the same values.
Section 9 onwards defines every deliverable. No artifact is read back as input to another artifact.

## 2. Sources and their authority

The exports overlap and conflict. Some values in them are wrong or superseded by design; the rules below decide which value counts.

- The four registry exports (registry_pypi.jsonl, registry_npm.csv, registry_maven.xml, registry_cargo.tsv) are authoritative for the
  releases of their ecosystem, their publication instants and their registry revisions. yank_notices.mbox carries yank and unyank
  notices and rename confirmations.
- registry_mirror.parquet is a mirror snapshot. It is not a source of releases, publication instants, yank state or licences. Its only
  authority is the quarantine flag: a release whose in-force mirror record says quarantined=true is withdrawn from the close entirely
  (it is not a release of its package at all).
- name_aliases.tsv is the package alias register. name_aliases_legacy.tsv is the retired crosswalk; it is authoritative only for alias
  rows of kind rename whose effective instant is before 2024-01-01T00:00:00Z, and those renames need no rename confirmation. Every other
  row of the retired crosswalk is ignored.
- The manifests (manifests_core.csv, manifests_edge.jsonl, manifests_data.yaml, manifests_legacy.txt) hold dependency declarations.
  A declaration counts only when it sits in the manifest named by its service's home manifest in service_catalog.xlsx (core, edge,
  data or legacy). manifests_snapshot.csv is a frozen copy of the core manifest; it is the source of declarations only for services
  whose service_catalog.xlsx lifecycle shows an archived event before the cut, and those services take every declaration from it.
- scanner_findings.jsonl holds container scans. It is the source of runtime pins only for services whose home manifest is image;
  for every other service its observations are ignored.
- service_catalog.xlsx is authoritative for services, tiers, regions, home manifests, ownership and lifecycle. service_catalog_final.xlsx
  is a preview export and is ignored, except its legacy_lifecycle sheet, which is the only lifecycle source for services migrated from
  the old inventory (services that have no lifecycle row in service_catalog.xlsx take their lifecycle from that sheet; the others ignore it).
- Deploy telemetry: deploy_events_east.log, deploy_events_west.log and deploy_events_central.log. deploy_events_legacy.log is the retired
  collector; its events count only for services in region us-central with a corrected event instant before 2025-08-04T00:00:00Z.
  Services in region global have no collector coverage.
- Advisories: advisories_osv.jsonl is authoritative for affected ranges and withdrawals. advisories_osv_replay.jsonl is a replayed feed;
  its events cell is used only for OSV records whose events cell carries the elsewhere sentinel, and nothing else in it counts.
  advisories_ghsa.json and advisories_nvd.xml carry vectors and publication data. advisories_feed_cache.json is an aggregator cache; it
  is used only for the vector of a GHSA record whose vector cell carries the elsewhere sentinel (the cache row of the same GHSA identifier
  and package), and its precomputed scores are never used.
- Every other export and document is authoritative for its own subject as described in the sections below.

## 3. Time

Timestamps are converted to UTC instants. The zones are:

| zone | rule |
|---|---|
| UTC | offset 0 |
| IE | UTC+0, UTC+1 from the last Sunday of March 01:00 UTC to the last Sunday of October 01:00 UTC |
| US | UTC-6, UTC-5 from the second Sunday of March 08:00 UTC to the first Sunday of November 07:00 UTC |
| IN | UTC+5:30 all year |
| JP | UTC+9 all year |
| AU | UTC+10, UTC+11 from 16:00 UTC on the Saturday before the first Sunday of October to 16:00 UTC on the Saturday before the first Sunday of April |

A wall-clock value in a zone is the UTC instant whose local time in that zone equals it. Exports never write wall-clock values inside a
transition hour. Calendar dates are proleptic Gregorian.

## 4. Records, revisions, errata and the cut

Every data row of every export (every table row of a document) is one revision of a record and carries four envelope fields: rid (record
identifier), rev (revision number, an integer), st (state) and rec_at (the instant the revision was recorded). The canonical states are
active, void and draft.

- Revisions of one record share rid within one table of one export. Two rows with the same rid and rev are the same revision written twice.
- A revision recorded at or after the cut is not applied by the close; it belongs to the deferred register (section 8.6).
- A draft revision never applies.
- Of the remaining revisions the one with the highest rev number is in force. When its state is void the record is void and does not
  exist for the close. A record with no remaining revision does not exist for the close.
- errata_register.docx holds errata. An erratum applies only when it was issued before the cut and its subject is approved
  (below). An applying erratum of action void voids its target record; one of action correct replaces the value of the named field of
  the target's in-force revision by the value written in canonical form (section 6). Errata of one target apply in order of issue
  instant, then rid; a later correction of the same field wins. The target is identified by file and rid.
- review_minutes.docx holds decisions. A subject (an erratum rid, a lock override rid or a waiver rid) is approved when, among its
  in-force minute rows decided before the cut, the one with the latest decided instant (then the highest rid) has decision approve.

For registry releases the history matters: the state of a release at an instant t is that of its latest applicable registry revision
recorded before t (applicable means: not draft, and only revisions recorded before the cut). The publication instant of a release is
the value of its revision in force at the cut, after errata.

## 5. Reading cells

### 5.1 Batches, layouts and the conventions register

Every data row carries a batch label (a batch column, key, attribute, header or table heading). In JSON Lines exports a line whose
only key is batch opens a batch and labels the rows that follow it. source_conventions.html is the
conventions register. Its rows are written in canonical form and follow the record rules of section 4. A register row names a file, a
batch and a field:

- conv is a convention name: the field's cells in that batch are written under that convention.
- conv @layout: token lists, in order, the column names the batch uses; conv lists the canonical field names in the same order.
- conv @unknown, @none or @elsewhere: token is the text that stands for that sentinel in that field of that batch.

A field with no in-force register row in a batch is written under its default convention. Default conventions: ts iso, date iso, dec
plain, int plain, hours h, money minor, bool tf, id canon, svcid canon, pkg canon, ver semver, vid canon, vec canon, spdx canon, events
semver, enumerations word. In manifests_legacy.txt a single dot stands for an empty cell. An empty cell is an absent value unless the
register gives it a sentinel meaning. A cell that is neither empty, nor a registered sentinel token, nor a value written in the
documented form of its convention (section 5.2) is invalid, and an input directory that carries one is invalid input (section 13); such a
cell is never read as an absent value. manifests_legacy.txt is encoded in Latin-1; registry_npm.csv, license_overrides.csv and
fx_fixings.tsv start with a UTF-8 byte order mark; every other text export is UTF-8.

Sentinels: unknown means the source records the value as unknown; none means there is deliberately no value (no withdrawal, no end
date, no revocation); elsewhere means the value is held by the export named in section 2 for that field.

### 5.2 Conventions

- ts: iso is YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ; off:Z is ISO 8601 with the numeric offset of zone Z; naive:Z is YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS wall clock of zone Z;
  epoch is Unix seconds; epochms is Unix milliseconds; xl:Z is an Excel serial day number (days since 1899-12-30, the fraction is the
  time of day) of the wall clock of zone Z; xl4:Z is the same counted from 1904-01-01; dmy:Z is DD/MM/YYYY HH:MM and mdy:Z is
  MM/DD/YYYY HH:MM, wall clock of zone Z. Serial values are rounded to the nearest minute.
- date: iso YYYY-MM-DD; dmy DD.MM.YYYY; mdy MM/DD/YYYY; compact YYYYMMDD; xl Excel serial day (days since 1899-12-30).
- dec: plain uses a dot decimal point; de uses dot thousands separators and a comma decimal point; fr uses space thousands separators and
  a comma decimal point.
- int: plain; grouped uses comma thousands separators; de uses dot thousands separators; neg writes the negated value.
- hours: h decimal hours; min a whole number of minutes (an integer); hm like 2h30m; iso ISO 8601 duration like PT2H30M.
- money (an amount in the currency of the row): minor is integer minor units; major is a decimal amount in major units; de is a major
  amount with dot thousands separators and comma decimal point; paren is a major amount with negatives in parentheses. Minor units:
  EUR, USD, GBP and INR have two decimals, JPY has none.
- bool: tf true/false; 10 1/0; yn yes/no; YN Y/N; neg writes the negation in true/false form.
- id: canon; upper upper case; spaced padded with spaces; under writes every hyphen as an underscore (identifiers never contain
  underscores).
- svcid (service identifiers): canon is svc- followed by five digits; upper upper case; nopad drops leading zeros of the number; spaced
  padded with spaces; legacy holds either a canonical identifier or a legacy identifier, resolved through the crosswalk in Appendix A:
  the crosswalk row of that legacy identifier whose window [valid_from, valid_to) contains the UTC date of the row's rec_at.
- pkg (package names): canon; case changes letter case; sep changes separators; fullwidth replaces a character by its full-width form;
  zw inserts a zero-width space. case and sep never apply to maven coordinates, which are case sensitive.
- ver: semver is MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH[-TAG.N]; native is the ecosystem spelling (pypi 1.4.0rc2, 1.4.0a1, 1.4.0b3; maven 1.4.0-RC2,
  1.4.0-alpha1, 1.4.0-beta3; npm and cargo as semver); vprefix is semver with a leading v; build is semver with +build metadata.
  Build metadata is never part of a version. TAG is one of alpha, beta, rc.
- vid: canon; lower lower case; nopad drops leading zeros of a CVE sequence number; spaced padded with spaces.
- vec: canon is a CVSS vector with its CVSS:3.0/ or CVSS:3.1/ prefix; noprefix drops the prefix of a CVSS:3.1 vector; lower lower case.
- spdx: canon; lower lower case; deprecated uses deprecated identifiers (GPL-2.0 for GPL-2.0-only, GPL-2.0+ for GPL-2.0-or-later and
  so on); alias uses the long names of license_policy.md.
- events: OSV events written kind:version separated by |, kinds introduced, fixed and last_affected, versions spelt semver or native.
- Enumerations: the word convention writes the canonical word; the other conventions are:

| vocabulary | convention | written=canonical |
|---|---|---|
| alias_kind | letter | R=rename, S=spelling, T=typo, V=vendor |
| dec | letter | Y=approve, N=reject, D=defer |
| eco | alt | pip=pypi, node=npm, mvn=maven, crates=cargo |
| eco | upper | PYPI=pypi, NPM=npm, MAVEN=maven, CARGO=cargo |
| err | upper | VOID=void, CORRECT=correct |
| home | upper | CORE=core, EDGE=edge, DATA=data, LEGACY=legacy, IMAGE=image |
| jkind | letter | O=opening, A=adjust, R=reversal |
| life | letter | A=archived, X=decommissioned, R=restored, P=paused, N=renamed |
| lstat | letter | D=decided, P=proposed, W=withdrawn |
| notice | upper | YANK=yank, UNYANK=unyank, RENAME=rename |
| outcome | alt | ok=succeeded, error=failed, rollback=rolled_back |
| outcome | letter | S=succeeded, F=failed, R=rolled_back |
| region | alt | euw1=eu-west, usc1=us-central, aps1=ap-south, apn1=ap-north, apse1=ap-southeast, glb=global |
| role | letter | O=owner, M=maintainer, R=reviewer |
| scope | alt | prod=runtime, development=dev, testing=test |
| scope | letter | R=runtime, D=dev, T=test |
| st | code | 1=active, 9=void, 0=draft |
| st | letter | A=active, V=void, D=draft |
| vex | alt | NA=not_affected, A=affected, F=fixed, UI=under_investigation |

## 6. Canonical forms

Canonical text of a value: timestamps iso, dates iso, decimals as plain decimals without trailing zeros, hours as decimal hours, integers
and money as integers (money in minor units), booleans true/false, versions semver without build metadata, OSV events with semver
versions, licence expressions in the canonical form of license_policy.md, enumerations as their word, service identifiers canonical,
sentinels as @ followed by their meaning (@unknown, @none, @elsewhere), absent values as the empty string, and every other value as the
text after the normalisations of section 7.

## 7. Identities

### 7.1 Services

A service is identified by its canonical identifier (section 5.2 svcid).

### 7.2 Packages

A package is identified by ecosystem:name with the name normalised as follows. All ecosystems: Unicode NFKC, then zero-width spaces
removed and surrounding spaces stripped. pypi: lower case, and every run of -, _ and . replaced by a single -. npm: lower case. cargo:
lower case and _ replaced by -. maven: nothing more (coordinates are case sensitive).

name_aliases.tsv maps an alias name to a target name within an ecosystem. Kind spelling: the alias is another name of the target
whenever its effective instant is before the cut. Kind rename: the alias is a former name of the target when the effective instant is
before the cut and a rename notice in yank_notices.mbox whose ref is the alias row's rid was sent before the cut (or, for the retired
crosswalk rows of section 2, with no notice). Kinds typo and vendor never merge names: a typo or vendor alias is a different package.
A name that is an alias in force maps to its target; releases published under a former name belong to the target once the rename is in
force, and otherwise form a package of their own.

### 7.3 Vulnerabilities

Vulnerability identifiers are normalised to upper case prefixes (CVE-, GHSA-, PYSEC-, RUSTSEC-, OSV-), GHSA bodies in lower case and CVE
sequence numbers with at least four digits. advisory_aliases.csv links identifiers; linked identifiers (transitively) are one
vulnerability. The canonical identifier of a vulnerability is its CVE identifier, otherwise its GHSA identifier, otherwise its OSV
identifier.

## 8. Rules of the close

### 8.1 Releases, yanks and resolution

A release of a package is an in-force registry record not withdrawn by the mirror quarantine. A yank notice sent at instant t sets the
release state yanked (unyank clears it) from t on; the yank state at an instant is given by the most recent of the latest applicable
registry revision recorded before the instant and the latest yank notice sent before the instant, a notice winning a tie.

A declaration's constraint is written in its ecosystem's syntax: npm ^ ~ x-ranges, comparator lists and exact versions; cargo bare
versions (caret), ^ ~ = * and comma-separated comparators; pypi == ~= .* and comma-separated comparators; maven exact [v], intervals
[a,b) (a,b] [a,) and bare versions, which are exact. Caret: ^M.m.p admits >=M.m.p <(M+1).0.0 when M>0, >=0.m.p <0.(m+1).0 when M=0 and
m>0, and >=0.0.p <0.0.(p+1) when M=m=0. Tilde ~M.m.p admits >=M.m.p <M.(m+1).0. pypi ~=M.m admits >=M.m.0 <(M+1).0.0 and ~=M.m.p admits
>=M.m.p <M.(m+1).0. M.m.x and M.m.* admit >=M.m.0 <M.(m+1).0. Missing version parts are zero. Version precedence is semantic versioning
precedence: a prerelease precedes its release, prerelease tags compare alpha < beta < rc and their numbers compare numerically. A
prerelease satisfies a constraint only when the constraint is an exact pin of it, or its lower bound is a prerelease of the same
MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.

The resolution instant of a declaration is its locked instant. The pinned version is the release of highest precedence that the
constraint admits, that was published before the resolution instant and that was not yanked at the resolution instant; an exact pin
may select a yanked release. When none qualifies the declaration is unsatisfiable.

A lock override in lock_overrides.toml forces the pinned version when it is approved (section 4), the UTC date 2025-09-30 lies in
[valid_from, valid_to), and the forced version is a release of the package published before the cut. Its disposition is overridden.
A declaration is in force for the close when its record is in force; declarations of services that are not active are ignored.

### 8.2 Services

A service's tier, region and home manifest come from its in-force service_catalog.xlsx row. Its owning team at the cut is the team of
its ownership row with the latest effective instant before the cut. Its status at the cut is decommissioned when, among its lifecycle
events at instants before the cut, the latest decommissioned or restored event is decommissioned; otherwise it is active. archived,
paused and renamed events do not change status.

Deploys: a deploy event's at value is the collector clock. clock_offsets.txt gives, per collector host and collector-clock UTC date of
at, the offset in seconds of the collector clock ahead of true time; the true instant is at minus the offset (an offset with no
in-force row is 0). An event is identified by
its rid across all collectors. A service's deploy count is the number of distinct in-force events with outcome succeeded and true instant
in the quarter, counted only from collectors with authority (section 2); it is null for a service with no collector coverage.

### 8.3 Vulnerabilities and exposures

An OSV record names the vulnerability by its OSV identifier and the affected package. A vulnerability is withdrawn when an in-force OSV
record of it carries a withdrawal instant before the cut. Its publication instant comes from the authority order for field published in
precedence.yaml: default osv then ghsa; an in-force precedence row for the vulnerability replaces the order. The first source in the order
that has an in-force record of the vulnerability gives the value, even when that value is the unknown sentinel (the publication is then
unknown). A vulnerability published at or after the cut is not part of the close.

Affected ranges: a version is affected by an OSV record when an introduced event (0 means from the start) precedes or equals it and
the next event after that introduced event is a fixed event greater than the version, or a last_affected event greater than or equal to
the version, or there is no next event.

The vector of a vulnerability: the latest approved rescore (advisory_rescores.parquet, approved true, scored before the cut) wins.
Otherwise the authority order for field cvss_vector (default nvd then ghsa, replaced by an in-force precedence row) picks the first
source with a vector. For nvd the in-force records of source NVD win over those of source CNA, and within a source the latest modified
instant wins. For ghsa the in-force GHSA record's vector applies. Base scores follow scoring_methodology.pdf.

An exposure is a runtime pin with a pinned version of an active service, and a vulnerability of the close (not withdrawn, published
before the cut or unknown) that affects that version through an OSV record of the pin's package. Its status is not_affected when the
latest vex_statements.xml statement for the service and vulnerability issued before the cut has status not_affected; otherwise waived
when a waiver in waivers.db for the service and vulnerability is approved, was granted before the cut and expires at or after the cut;
otherwise open. Statuses affected, fixed and under_investigation of a statement change nothing.

days_open, sla_days, breach and estimate follow sla_policy.pdf and scoring_methodology.pdf. The figures those documents state
(the targets by severity and tier, the holidays by region, the base hours by severity and the tier weights) are facts of the corpus:
they are read from the documents of the input directory, and another corpus may state different figures. fixed is the release of lowest precedence above
the pinned version that is published before the cut, not yanked at the cut and not affected; null when there is none.

### 8.4 Licences

The licence of a release is the expression of the latest approved licence override (license_overrides.csv, status decided, decided
before the cut) whose range admits the release, ranges admitting prereleases too; otherwise the expression of the latest scan of the
release scanned before the cut; otherwise NOASSERTION. Its obligation class follows license_policy.md, unless an exception in
license_policy.md whose range admits the release is granted on or before 2025-09-30 and not revoked on or before 2025-09-30, in which
case the class is the exception's class (the latest granted exception wins).

### 8.5 Teams and the ledger

Teams are the in-force teams rows of engineer_roster.ini. Capacity of a team of model capped is the floor of the sum, over its in-force
allocations, of fte x 8 x the number of Monday-to-Friday UTC dates of the quarter inside [start, end) x (1 - oncall); uncapped teams have no
capacity (null). A team's engineers are the distinct engineers with an in-force allocation overlapping the quarter.

Ledger amounts are EUR cents. A timesheet entry costs hours x the engineer's hourly rate (engineer_rates.xlsx, for the calendar month of
worked; months after September use the September rate) converted to EUR, as a spend (negative). Journal entries convert their amount.
Conversion uses the latest in-force fixing of fx_fixings.tsv at or before the instant (EUR per unit; EUR converts at 1); the EUR amount
is rounded once, half to even, to whole cents. A timesheet entry belongs to the team of the engineer's in-force allocation covering the
UTC date of worked. A journal reversal that is in force cancels itself and the entry it reverses. Openings are the journal entries of kind
opening. The closing balance of a team is its openings plus all its in-force entries booked before the cut.

### 8.6 Controls, custody and the deferred register

control_totals.html publishes control figures. Its in-force rows are carried into the stage-1 ledger as published.

Custody of an export: records is its number of data rows; in_force is the number of records in force at the cut after errata;
received_crc is the CRC-32 (zlib) of the concatenation, in written order, of every data row's canonical line followed by a line feed;
inforce_crc is the same over the canonical lines of the in-force revisions after errata, ordered by table in the order the export
defines them and then by rid. A canonical line joins with | the rid, the rev, the canonical state, the canonical rec_at and the canonical
text of every other field in the order the export defines them (for errata-corrected fields the corrected text). Written order is file
order, sheets and tables in their stored order.

The deferred register lists every non-draft revision recorded at or after the cut in advisory_rescores.parquet, remediation_timesheets.csv,
ledger_journal.db, waivers.db, vex_statements.xml and lock_overrides.toml. action is new when the record has no applicable revision, void
for a void revision, revise otherwise. For rescores the target is the canonical vulnerability; measure is cvss when the revision is
approved and not void (current: the vulnerability's base score as of the cut; amended: the score of the revision's vector), otherwise
none. For timesheets and journal entries the target is the team (timesheets: the team of the allocation covering the revision's worked
date) and measure is cents (current: the entry's amount as of the cut, null when the entry is not in force or cancelled; amended: the
amount under the revision, null when the revision is void; post-cut instants convert at the latest in-force fixing). For the other
three exports the target is the service and the measure none.

## 9. Deliverables: general rules

Stage 1, stage 2 and stage 3 name the source, risk and close layers; "the stage-1 files" and "the stage-2 files" mean the source-layer
and risk-layer artifacts exactly as this run serialises them. The program writes all twenty-four artifacts into its output directory.
Text is UTF-8 with line feeds and a final line feed.

- JSON Lines files hold one JSON object per line with the keys in the order listed; no spaces outside strings.
- JSON documents are one object with the keys in the order listed, in the two-space layout: an object or list opens on the line of its
  key, each member follows on its own line indented two spaces deeper than the line that opened it ("key": value in objects), members
  are separated by a comma at the end of the line, and the closing brace or bracket stands alone at the indentation of the opening
  line; an empty object or list is {} or [], and an object inside a list is written on one line exactly as in JSON Lines.
- Absent values are null. Timestamps are canonical iso, dates YYYY-MM-DD, versions semver.
- A decimal field is a JSON number written with exactly the listed number of decimals, rounded half to even at that step. Downstream
  stages compute from the decimal values as written upstream, never from a fresh derivation.
- Every stage-2 document carries upstream: an object whose keys are "p1/" followed by the file name of each of the six stage-1 files
  (for example p1/packages.jsonl), in stage-1 file order, each mapped to the lower-case hexadecimal SHA-256 of that file's exact bytes.
  Every stage-3 document carries upstream with the same six p1/ keys followed by "p2/" keys for the six stage-2 files (for example
  p2/package_risk.json), twelve keys in all.
- The stage-1 sequence is the concatenation, in this order, of packages.jsonl, pins.jsonl, exposures.jsonl, services.jsonl,
  teams.csv and ledger.jsonl, each in written row order. Ordered computations below walk it.
- A written null and a written zero are different facts: null is absent or unknown, zero is a measured zero.
- Audit rows. In the rules below, "one row per ..." counts data rows. After its data rows every stage-1 JSON Lines file carries 1,000
  audit lines, every stage-2 and stage-3 document carries an evidence key, and teams.csv carries 1,000 audit rows. Readers of an
  artifact skip audit lines and audit rows. They are defined in section 12.7.

## 10. Stage 1

### 10.1 packages.jsonl

One row per package that has at least one release published before the cut, ordered by package. Keys: package (ecosystem:name),
ecosystem, owner_team (team of the latest owner-role row of package_owners.json effective before the cut; null when none), releases
(releases published before the cut), yanked (of those, yanked at the cut), latest (highest-precedence release that is not a prerelease
and not yanked at the cut; null when none), latest_license (licence of latest, canonical; null when latest is null), obligation (class of
latest_license for that release; null when latest is null).

### 10.2 pins.jsonl

One row per in-force declaration of an active service from its authoritative source, plus one row per scanner observation of an image
service (the latest scan before the cut of each package), ordered by service, package, scope. Keys: service, package, scope, constraint
(the canonical interval: =V for exact pins, otherwise >= or > lower bound and < or <= upper bound separated by one space; null for scanner
rows), source (file name of the declaration's export), pinned (version or null), disposition (pinned, unsatisfiable, overridden or
observed), license and obligation of the pinned release for runtime pins (null for other scopes and when pinned is null).

### 10.3 exposures.jsonl

One row per exposure, ordered by service, package, vuln. Keys: service, package, version, vuln (canonical), cvss (1 decimal), severity,
status, published (the local date of the publication instant in the zone of the service's region; null when unknown), days_open (integer
or null), sla_days (integer), breach (boolean), estimate (2 decimals), fixed (version or null).

### 10.4 services.jsonl

One row per service of service_catalog.xlsx, ordered by service. Keys: service, team, tier, region, status, deploys (integer or null),
weight (4 decimals, scoring_methodology.pdf).

### 10.5 teams.csv

Header team,model,capacity_hours,engineers, one row per team ordered by team; an empty capacity_hours cell is null.

### 10.6 ledger.jsonl

Rows in this order: opening rows (ordered by team), entry rows (ordered by booked then entry), closing rows (ordered by team, one per team),
control rows (ordered by control), custody rows (ordered by file, one per export), deferred rows (ordered by recorded then amendment).

- opening: kind, team, entry (journal rid), amount_cents.
- entry: kind, entry (rid), team, source (file name), booked (worked or booked instant), amount_cents. Openings and cancelled entries are
  not entry rows.
- closing: kind, team, amount_cents.
- control: kind, control, scope, measure, value.
- custody: kind, file, records, in_force, received_crc, inforce_crc (CRCs as eight lower-case hexadecimal digits).
- deferred: kind, amendment (file#rid#rev), file, rid, rev, recorded, action, target, measure, current, amended (cvss values with 1
  decimal, cents as integers, null when absent).

## 11. Stage 2 (derived only from the six stage-1 files)

Open exposures are the exposures rows with status open, in written order. The weight of an exposure is the weight of its service row.
risk sums cvss x weight over the rows concerned. share is a value divided by the total of the same value over all rows of the
list (0 when the total is 0). dense_rank ranks by the named value descending, equal values sharing a rank, ranks consecutive.
pct_rank is (number of rows with a smaller value) / (rows - 1), 0 for a single row.

### 11.1 package_risk.json

Keys: upstream, rows, totals. rows: one per package with at least one open exposure, ordered by package: package, open, max_cvss (1 decimal),
risk (6), share (6, of risk), pct_rank (6, of risk), dense_rank (of risk). totals: packages, risk (6, sum of the rows' risk).

### 11.2 service_risk.json

Keys: upstream, rows, totals. rows: one per active service in services.jsonl order: service, team, open, risk (6), share (6), dense_rank,
breaches (open exposures with breach true). totals: services, risk (6).

### 11.3 remediation_plan.json

Keys: upstream, rows, totals. rows: one per open exposure in stage-1 sequence order: seq (1-based), service, package, vuln, severity,
estimate (2), team (the service's team), team_hours (2), pool_hours (2), unallocated (2), team_remaining (2, null for teams without
capacity), pool_remaining (2). The fold: each team starts with its capacity_hours (null means unlimited, zero means none); the pool starts
with the value of the stage-1 control row of scope global and measure pool_hours. Only the first 6 open exposures of each
service, in sequence order, are eligible; others get nothing. An eligible row takes team_hours = the estimate capped by the team's
remaining hours (all of it for an unlimited team); a critical or high row then takes pool_hours = the rest capped by the remaining pool.
unallocated is what is left. totals: team_hours, pool_hours, unallocated (2 decimals each), pool_cap (integer).

### 11.4 team_rollup.json

Keys: upstream, rows. rows: one per teams.csv row in order: team, services (active services of the team), open, risk (6), share (6),
dense_rank (of risk), balance, held, held_entries. Ledger fold: walk the stage-1 ledger rows in written order; a team's balance starts
at the sum of its opening rows; an entry row that would take its team's balance below zero is held (added to held, counted in
held_entries) and leaves the balance unchanged; any other entry row is added to the balance.

### 11.5 license_summary.json

Keys: upstream, classes, teams. classes: one per class in the order permissive, weak, strong, network, unknown, over runtime pins with a
pinned version: class, pins, packages (distinct packages), share (6, of pins). teams: one per teams.csv row: team, strong (runtime pins
of the team's services with obligation strong or network), dense_rank (of strong).

### 11.6 sla_metrics.json

Keys: upstream, severities. One row per severity in the order critical, high, medium, low, none, over open exposures: severity, open,
with_days (rows with a days_open value), nulls (rows with null days_open), zeros (rows with days_open 0), breaches, mean_days (6, over the
days_open values; null without values), p50 and p90 (nearest rank: the value at position ceil(p x n) of the sorted values; null without
values), max_days (null without values).

## 12. Stage 3 (derived only from the six stage-1 and six stage-2 files)

### 12.1 release_gates.json

Keys: upstream, rows, totals. rows: one per active service in services.jsonl order: service, team, verdict, reasons. Reasons, listed in
this order when they hold: critical_breach (an open exposure of the service is critical with breach true), top_risk (the service's
risk is above zero and its service_risk dense_rank is at most ceil(0.02 x the number of service_risk rows)),
unsatisfiable_pin (a runtime pin of the service is unsatisfiable), unfunded_remediation (a critical or high remediation_plan row of the
service has unallocated above zero), network_license (a runtime pin of the service has obligation network). verdict is block when
critical_breach or top_risk holds, hold when another reason holds, pass otherwise. totals: block, hold, pass.

### 12.2 control_reconciliation.json

Keys: upstream, rows, totals. rows: one per stage-1 control row ordered by control: control, scope, measure, published (the control
value), stage1, stage2, agrees. stage1 and stage2 recompute the measure (null when the stage has no counterpart):
open_exposures of a team, service or package: stage1 counts open exposures of the scope in exposures.jsonl, stage2 reads open of the
scope's team_rollup, service_risk or package_risk row (0 when absent); runtime_pins: stage1 counts runtime pins with a pinned version;
closing_cents: stage1 the closing row, stage2 balance plus held of team_rollup; capacity_hours: stage1 teams.csv; records and in_force:
stage1 the custody row of the file; pool_hours: stage2 the remediation_plan pool_cap; total_open: stage1 the number of open exposures,
stage2 the sum of package_risk open; waived_total: stage1 the number of waived exposures. agrees is true when every non-null stage
value equals published and at least one is non-null. totals: controls, agreeing.

### 12.3 deferred_amendments.json

Keys: upstream, rows, totals. rows: one per stage-1 deferred row in written order: amendment, file, target, measure, current, amended,
impact, after. For cvss rows with both values: impact (6) is the sum over open exposures of the target vulnerability of (amended - current)
x weight, after (6) is the package_risk total risk plus impact. For cents rows: impact is amended minus current (nulls as 0), after is the
team's closing plus impact (null without a target). Otherwise both are null. totals: amendments, cvss_impact (6), cents_impact.

### 12.4 team_scorecard.json

Keys: upstream, rows. rows: one per teams.csv row: team, risk (team_rollup), capacity (teams.csv), allocated (2, the team's
remediation_plan team_hours), utilization (6, allocated / capacity; null when capacity is null or 0), breaches (sum of the team's
service_risk breaches), closing (the closing row), balance (team_rollup), score (6, risk x (1 + breaches / (team_rollup open + 1))),
dense_rank (of score).

### 12.5 certification.json

Keys: upstream, summary. summary: active_services, runtime_pins, open_exposures, critical_breaches (open critical exposures with breach),
risk_stage1 (6, sum of cvss x weight over open exposures), risk_packages (6, sum of package_risk row risks), risk_services (6, sum of
service_risk row risks), gap_packages (6, risk_packages - risk_stage1), gap_services (6), pool_used (remediation_plan totals pool_hours),
unallocated (remediation_plan totals unallocated), estimate_total (2, sum of remediation_plan estimates), verdict: certified when
critical_breaches is 0 and unallocated is 0, qualified when unallocated is at most 0.05 x estimate_total, withheld otherwise.

### 12.6 chain_audit.json

Keys: upstream, files, checks. files: one per upstream file in stage order (six stage-1 files, then six stage-2 files): file (p1/ or p2/
followed by the file name), rows (stage-1 data rows; for stage-2 documents the length of rows, or of classes or severities), bytes. checks, in this order,
each check and passed: package_risk_covers_open_packages, service_risk_covers_active_services, plan_covers_open_exposures,
team_rollup_covers_teams, license_pins_match, sla_open_matches, risk_totals_agree (package and service risk totals within 0.0000005 per
row of both lists), ledger_balances_close (balance plus held equals closing for every team).


### 12.7 Audit rows and evidence views

Digest and fold. A digest is lower-case hexadecimal SHA-256 text. For a digest D and a slot i, fold(D, i) is the integer value of the
eight hexadecimal digits of D that start at character position (i mod 56), counting from 0, plus i, all modulo 1000003.

Audit lines of the stage-1 JSON Lines files. D is the SHA-256 of the file's data lines (each line with its line feed). For i from 0
through 999 the file then carries one line {"_evidence":{"lineage":{"artifact":{"name":F,"digest":D,"slot":i,"fold":fold(D,i)}},
"relation_web":{"locators":{"edge_00":L0, ... ,"edge_79":L79}}}}, written like every other line of the file, where F is the file name
and Lj is the first 20 hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the text F:D:i:j (i and j written as plain integers, edge keys with two
digits).

Audit rows of teams.csv. D is the SHA-256 of the team data rows written as JSON Lines with the keys team, model, capacity_hours and
engineers. For i from 0 through 999 the file then carries the row audit/NNNN,lineage/E/K,G,M where NNNN is i with four digits, E is the
first 12 digits of D, K is i mod 17, G is (the integer value of the first eight digits of D plus i) mod 1000003, and M is i + 1.

Evidence of the stage-2 and stage-3 documents. Each document ends with the key evidence: a list of 1,000 objects, for i from 0 through
999 {"_evidence":{"lineage":{"artifact":{"name":F,"digest":D,"slot":i,"fold":fold(D,i)}}}}, where F is the file name and D is the
SHA-256 of the document written without its evidence key.

Evidence views. The six evidence files have 1,200 entries ordered by slot from 0 through 1199. Every entry carries its slot, its
lineage, the artifact name global-close, the close digest C, fold(C, slot), open_count and risk. C is the SHA-256 of the document
{"p1": {...}, "p2": {...}, "p3": {...}} in the two-space layout of section 9, where p1 maps each stage-1 file name to its list of data
rows, and p2 and p3 map each stage-2 and stage-3 file name to its document, all in stage file order and without audit rows or evidence
keys; it is the same in every entry. open_count is the number of open exposures
and risk is the certification risk_stage1 value with six decimals. The identity is the slot plus lineage. The layouts are:

- authority-trace.xml: the line <?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>, the line <close><authority><trace><rows>, one line per entry
  <row><identity><slot>S</slot></identity><source><name>global-close</name><digest>C</digest></source><value><fold>F</fold>
  <open_count>O</open_count><risk>R</risk></value></row> written without spaces between elements, and the line
  </rows></trace></authority></close>.
- rollup-ledger.tsv: the header slot, parent_id, lineage, artifact, digest, fold, open_count, risk separated by tabs, then one row per
  entry: S, root-NN where NN is slot mod 31 with two digits, close/authority/trace/S/value, global-close, C, F, O, R.
- reconciliation-map.yaml: the lines close:, reconciliation:, map: and entries: indented by two spaces per level, then per entry
  "- slot: S" at eight spaces, lineage: at ten, authority: at twelve, name: global-close and digest: C at fourteen, result: at fourteen,
  and fold: F, open_count: O and risk: R at sixteen.
- dependency-matrix.ini: per entry the section [close.reconciliation.entry.S.authority.result] followed by the keys name, digest, fold,
  open_count and risk, written key=value. The first 216 entries also carry dependent, prerequisite, locator_identity (the name),
  locator_digest (the digest) and locator_fold (the fold). The 216 dependency edges list, for each artifact A[d] of the order six
  stage-1 files, six stage-2 files, six stage-3 files, authority-trace.xml, rollup-ledger.tsv, reconciliation-map.yaml,
  dependency-matrix.ini, freeze-register.log, training-closure.md (d from 0 through 23), the prerequisites A[p] for p from
  max(0, d - w) through d - 1, where w is 13 when d is 13 through 18 and 12 otherwise; entry k carries the k-th edge as dependent A[d]
  and prerequisite A[p].
- freeze-register.log: one line per entry: level=INFO correlation=close-NN (slot mod 29, two digits) span=authority-SSSS (slot, four
  digits) path=close/reconciliation/entry/S/result artifact=global-close digest=C fold=F open_count=O risk=R.
- training-closure.md: the line # Closure evidence, an empty line, the header | slot | path | artifact | digest | fold | open | risk |,
  the alignment line |---:|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|, then one row per entry | S | close/reconciliation/entry/S/result |
  global-close | C | F | O | R |.

All are UTF-8 with LF endings and a final newline.

## 13. The program

The complete close is produced by one invocation:

    python3 build.py DATA_DIR OUT_DIR

The program reads only DATA_DIR, computes all layers in memory, creates OUT_DIR, and writes the complete set there. It never reads back a
file it wrote. Entries of OUT_DIR that are not artifacts of the close (the program itself, for example) are left in place. The set may
be staged in a private temporary directory beside OUT_DIR and published in one step. Exit status 0 means success; 2 means wrong arguments; 3 means an input is missing, unreadable or invalid. An error writes
nothing and prints exactly one line beginning "error: " to standard error. Output is deterministic, idempotent and independent of directory
names. Every artifact uses UTF-8, LF endings and a final newline. JSON null is the null form; CSV and TSV use an empty cell.
